# OPS-17 — the govtech stack: plan of record

## Why this document exists

Four lanes (Smart Files, SmartCity/Bastrop, Plan Review, ICC) are about to be built by four hand-carried lane planners. They share a spine, an auth leg, an atom contract, and a customer. Without a frozen baseline with stable row IDs, they will drift into four private definitions of done and meet at integration.

This repo has a measured base rate: prose-shaped controls run 0-for-3, structure-shaped controls run 1-for-1. This document is the structure-shaped answer, copied in shape from OPS-16, which proved it on the Smart Site program. The spine unification handoff states the cost of adopting it late: Smart Site adopted this machinery four days in and spent those four days paying for its absence. This program adopts it at hour zero.

**Definition of done for the program.** All four lanes complete through Layer 5, the govtech stack built and tested, the spine healthy, and each surface consuming and depositing into the others per the seam table below. Every row graded by its named instrument. A row graded by narration or doc assertion fails.

## Governing rules

These are the OPS-16 rules, which govern here unchanged except where noted. `90_runbooks/AGENT_CONTRACT.md` is the operative law for lane behavior and is not restated here.

1. **The baseline table never gets edited.** Every scope change is a dated row in the AMENDMENTS table. What we believed on 2026-08-14 stays visible forever.
2. **No dispatch without a row.** Every dispatch names `PLAN-ROW: G-xx`. Row IDs carry a `G-` prefix so they never collide with the OPS-16 `P-` rows in a grep, a hook, or a compiler.
3. **Statuses re-grade only by instrument** (SQL, gh, live probe, script). The GRADE LOG records each pass.
4. **Lane-planner fan model** per AGENT_CONTRACT section 1. Four lane planners, hand-carried. Verification never delegated below the lane planner.
5. **Write-slot law** per AGENT_CONTRACT section 3. This program shares the atoms store with any other running program; only `--apply` queues, everything else is parallel.
6. **Shared-leg rows block across lanes.** A row in the SHARED LEGS table is not owned by one lane. No lane may build past a shared leg it depends on by stubbing it privately.

## The structure: four lanes by five layers

The five layers are the proven OPS-16 ladder, generalized. They are ordered by what-blocks-what, not by subject:

**L1 Foundation** — what must exist for anything else to be true.
**L2 Measurement** — does it actually cover what we claim, counted by instrument.
**L3 Integrity** — is it honest where it does not cover, and is access enforced.
**L4 Depth** — jurisdiction- and customer-specific richness.
**L5 Launch** — the commercial surface: priced, sellable, demonstrable.

The four lanes are subjects, each passing through all five layers:

| Lane | Subject | Twin relationship | Primary repo | Owner | Sequence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **A** | Smart Files | the twin DOCUMENTS (a **NEW** atom family, per A-012) | legacy-design-tools | **nick** + planning agent | **FIRST** (A-007) |
| **B** | SmartCity / Bastrop | a PORTFOLIO of twins including infrastructure | smartcity-os (**absolute no-touch on live Bastrop production**; see CTRL-2) | **nick** + planning agent | last (consumes A, C, Smart Site) |
| **C** | Plan Review | the twin ADJUDICATIONS (the earning loop) | legacy-design-tools (cortex-reporting, ADR-023) | **nick** + planning agent | parallel to A |
| **D** | ICC | a licensed SOURCE metered across every twin citing it | hauska-engine | **nick** + planning agent | parallel to A |

**Owner rule (A-006).** A lane or shared leg without a named owner is not dispatchable. The operator assigns owners at dispatch time; "unassigned" is a blocking state, not a default. **Assigned 2026-08-14: nick owns every lane and shared leg, with a hand-carried planning agent executing each.** The owner is who is accountable, not who does the work; the lane planner is named at dispatch.

**WDLL card rule (A-006).** Every lane carries a FROZEN WDLL card (What Done Looks Like), written at dispatch time and never edited afterward — the lane's own definition of done, in its own words, against which drift is visible. It is filed with the dispatch and cited in the close, with a graded Finish card at close. Full practice: `90_runbooks/wdll_practice.md`. Never call it an "acceptance card" — one artifact with two names is how two lanes produce non-comparable closes.

## The twin, stated once

Ratified by the operator 2026-08-14 and consistent with `65_sensors/sensor_program_overview.md`:

**A twin is a node. A node is an ID full of atom facts. The ID can be a human, a building, a desk.** What this company specializes in is creating twins of things with provenance and access control. That is the essence; everything in this program is a form of it.

Degrees of aliveness (ratified 2026-08-06): a COLD twin has the record; a WARM twin has it verified with provenance on every fact; a LIVE twin has senses streaming current state. The three verbs are CAPTURE, CONNECT, SENSE, in that order, as a cost discipline: hardware is the last resort.

**Sensors are Asset Management Tier 2, not a separate program.** The `_smartcity_masters/32_smartcity_asset_management.md` tiers (record, live state, view) and the sensor program cold/warm/live ladder are the same architecture at two altitudes. The sensor band adds the WATCH plane (a standing rule on a live twin: baseline plus condition plus roster) and the hardware doctrine.

**The two-altitude vocabulary rule is binding on every artifact this program produces.** Internally: twin, node, atom, graph. Externally to a city: the record, the asset, current state. Never say digital twin to a city. The Asset Management master carries the full never-say list; the Smart Files master carries the foundation one-sentence rule and its own never-say list. A claim not in the approved-claims register of a master is not approved.

## Inherited spine constraints (compile-time requirements, not advice)

From `_inbox/2026-08-14_spine_unification_handoff.md`. Every lane inherits these; no lane re-derives them. A lane deliverable that violates one is rejected at checkpoint, not at launch.

1. **Absence is typed and provenanced.** The seven-status taxonomy exists because one undifferentiated absent carried at least seven meanings. Plan Review and ICC face the identical class: code section not found must never conflate never-looked, source-down, paywalled, and genuinely-absent.
2. **Only a positive determination writes an absence.** An empty result re-enters the queue. This one rule killed an entire defect class.
3. **satisfied-absent is a first-class product state.** A surface that cannot render verified-absent misrepresents the substrate.
4. **Freshness is part of every response.** Visible computedAt and servedAt stamps, and a STALE indicator proven able to fire before it is trusted. This binds hardest on Smart Files: it is an artifact store, and a cache without a stamp is a liar waiting for load.
5. **Vintages are declared, never mixed; fallbacks are named, counted, and marked on every fallback read.** Plan Review hits this with code editions (the Bastrop B3-repealed-to-BDC incident is the same disease); ICC citations carry edition identity for the same reason.
6. **EntityId shapes are NOT uniform across families, by design.** Never reconstruct an identity from parts; use the value storage persists. A wrong reconstruction silently matches zero rows and looks like an honest absence.
7. **County-level and portfolio-level determinations are a legitimate node class.** Any surface that aggregates must know the marker convention exists.
8. **Ingestion is a Factory 1.5 workload.** Smart Files ingest and plan-review corpus intake reuse the staging pattern; they do not invent parallel machinery.
9. **Instruments are build items.** Gates are scripts. A gating indicator is tested for its ability to fire before it is trusted. Every ratio travels with its counting rule.

## SHARED LEGS — cross-lane blocking dependencies

Not owned by any single lane. A lane that needs one may not stub it privately.

| ID | Leg | Why it is shared | Blocks | Owner |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| S-1 | **Auth and tenancy** (sprint-54 leg) | The claim flow is the single most load-bearing dependency in the convergence (65_sensors convergence doc). Tenant isolation is not enforced today; Cortex runs an anonymous default tenant. | B (Bastrop private layer), A (per-tenant artifacts), C (reviewer identity), the claim flow | **nick** + planning agent — longest pole |
| S-2 | **Telemetry plane placement** | Open item 1 in the sensor overview: named, not decided. Needs the ADR-008 and 56 target-topology check before it hardens. | B Tier 2 (live state), any watch work | **nick** + planning agent |
| S-3 | **Smart Site mapping adoption** | Operator-named. The Smart Site mapping system is the proven one; SmartCity adopts it and Bastrop mapping comes up to that standard. | B, C (E6 map compose) | **nick** + planning agent |
| S-4 | **ICC content-to-actor reference** | ICC is metered across BOTH Smart Site and Plan Review. The meter currently detects ICC content by allowlist and regex heuristic, not a hard reference. Two citing surfaces, one ledger. | D, C (F6 code library), any Smart Site ICC citation | **nick** + planning agent (lane D executes) |
| S-5 | **Consumer contract shape** | ADR-023 rules that SmartCity consumes plan review as a FUNCTION. Whether all three suppliers are consumed via MCP, direct atom read, or service API must be one answer, not three. | B consumes A, C, and Smart Site | **nick** + planning agent |
| S-6 | **Twin node-class and entityId ruling** | A twin of a human, a building, a desk, or a pump station is not the parcel-keyed shape. Constraint 6 says shapes are non-uniform by design; this program must DECLARE its shapes rather than discover them. **Now also carries the A-007 ruling: does Smart Files EXTEND the brokerage workspace family or SUPERSEDE it?** | A, B (assets), any sensor work | **nick** + planning agent — RULED (A-012) |

## The seam table — what consumes and deposits into what

Operator-stated 2026-08-14. This is the definition of done for all apps consuming and depositing into each other.

| Seam | Direction | Ruling and status |
|---|---|---|
| SmartCity consumes Smart Files | B from A | Documents are the twin records; SmartCity is a consumer, never a second implementation |
| SmartCity consumes Plan Review | B from C | ADR-023: consumer pass only, after the C standalone gate passes. No review logic duplicated in SmartCity |
| SmartCity consumes Smart Site | B from Smart Site | Mapping adoption (S-3) plus the public record layer beneath the city own |
| ICC paid across Smart Site AND Plan Review | D from both | Two citing surfaces, one ledger, one actor identity. Requires S-4 |
| Twins (freeze watch class) surface on Smart Site | Smart Site from B and sensors | Already ruled: the sensor program IS the live rung of the Smart Site ladder (convergence doc, 2026-08-06) |
| Bastrop infra private to Bastrop, controlled via Smart Site, blocked to public | B | Already ruled: city view equals city own sensors plus public layer; landlord data never feeds a city dashboard. Requires S-1 |
| Plan Review deposits adjudications as atoms | C to spine | The earning loop. Structural commitment 2 depends on it |

Mechanism, all four facts verified at source: `web/index.html` hardcodes `<section class="lens on" id="lens-city-manager">`, so Overview is ON in the static document; `shell.css` hides lenses with `.lens { display: none }` and shows with `.lens.on`; the nav items are real `<a href="/?lens=...">` links so every click is a FULL PAGE NAVIGATION; and `app.js` loads as `<script type="module">`, which is deferred by definition and therefore runs after the document parses. So the browser paints Overview, which is the lens carrying real content, and only then does `applyLens()` switch to the requested lens. On a not-built lens the operator sees a populated screen collapse into an honest-empty one, which reads as a broken deploy. **NOT caused by the G-88 deploy, and that was established rather than asserted:** `index.html` and `app.js` are byte-identical across `cdfca39..6a4580d`, the `class="lens on"` count is 1 on both sides, and revision `00017-vx4` was tagged `pre` at 0% traffic so the operator could A/B the pre-deploy revision directly and reproduce it there. The deploy plausibly WIDENED the window without creating it: `shell.css` grew 27 KB to 37.6 KB and is render-blocking, and the first load after the deploy had a cold cache on every asset because the service had never sent a validator before. Both delay `app.js` relative to first paint. **The fix cannot be CSS alone and cannot be a script move.** A module script is always deferred, so no reordering makes it run before paint, and CSS cannot read a query string. The shape that works without a build step, which this repo does not have and should not gain: an inline classless script in `<head>` that reads `location.search` and stamps `data-lens` on the root element, plus ONE rule keying visibility off that attribute rather than off a class JS adds later, so visibility is decided before first paint and the flash is structurally impossible rather than merely faster. Attribute-based, so the G-88 item 3 class gate stays satisfied. **Done means the flash cannot recur, not that it looks better:** the acceptance instrument is a probe that fails on the CURRENT build, since a fix nobody watched fail is the pattern this program keeps paying for. Related defect to fold in or row separately: the static document's default `.on` is itself the bug surface, and any fix must keep every lens reachable with JS disabled or state plainly that it does not. Do not start G-52. Do not fill G-24. Live Bastrop no-touch. L26 untouched. | Operator: found it on the deployed surface, confirmed the diagnosis. | nick (found); planner (diagnosis, scope) |

| A-075 | 2026-08-19 | **Three process findings, each measured, each with teeth beyond this row.** **(1) A green check-run plus a `CLEAN` merge state is NOT a merge gate**, demonstrated twice inside one hour. A check-run conclusion is a statement about the PR's own tree, and `CLEAN` is a statement about textual conflict; neither says anything about the tree that exists after the merge, and none of the three SmartCity repos requires branches to be up to date before merging. Merging G-89 broke PR #24 (218 tests, 215 pass, **3 fail**, from two PRs that share no source file) and simultaneously invalidated kit PR #7, whose CI checks `smartcity-dashboards` out at `ref: main` so its green was a fact about a world that no longer existed. Both would have shipped red on their own greens. The standing AGENT_CONTRACT rule — merge only on the conclusion STRING — is necessary and is not sufficient; the addition is to re-green against the CURRENT base before merging, and for a cross-repo consumer the base that matters is every repo its CI reads, not only its own. The preferred mechanization is branch protection's up-to-date requirement, because a control that depends on the merging agent remembering is the shape DEV_PROCESS rule 0 exists to name. Environment note: `git rebase` is blocked in this environment's Bash tool, so `gh api -X PUT repos/<owner>/<repo>/pulls/<n>/update-branch` is the form to reach for. Detail `_inbox/2026-08-19_finding_green_ci_plus_clean_is_not_a_merge_gate.md`. **(2) WDLL item 10's acceptance walk cannot see what it is written to check.** The served document is byte-identical across every lens and every pack — six URL forms, one sha256 — because `index.html` is one static document carrying every lens inline and the pack differences arrive later through `/api/*`. Item 10 as written is thirteen identical GETs and then thirteen more, and would return the same result on a redesigned build and an untouched one. Replaced with three legs: a deployed-bytes hash against the merged tree, pack checks moved to `/api/*` where the packs actually differ, and a real browser walk, which is the only leg that can grade a design pass. Precedent for re-shaping an acceptance item on a measurement: item 8 was re-sequenced at A-071 and the operator accepted. Detail `_inbox/2026-08-19_g88_item10_walk_is_vacuous.md`. **(3) The two defects that mattered most were found by planner verification AFTER CI was green, and both were in an INSTRUMENT rather than in a product.** The addressability gate's created-by-script derivation recognised `X.dataset.Y =` but not `setAttribute("data-...")`, so `data-surface` read as missing while `data-tab` and `data-atab` escaped only because they already exist in static markup — passing by luck rather than by rule. And the gate unioned the served documents with the BAKE SOURCE, so detaching `data-disposition` from `web/index.html` alone left it green at 10 pass and 0 fail while the 446-element connections register lost its entire severity colouring. Both were closed by widening a DERIVATION rather than by adding an exclusion, plus a pinned excused set so the derivation cannot self-heal into silence. | A-093 | 2026-09-02 | **Production engagement-creation break FIXED.** Root cause was not a code defect: `plan-review-00016-buh` (deployed by the G-107 session) was built via `gcloud run deploy --source .` from a local checkout that had G-107's own merge but had not fetched G-108's concurrently-landed merge (git commit order on `origin/main` is #7 → #8/G-109 → #9/G-107 → #10/G-108 — G-107 merged before G-108, and the deploy ran from whatever the local working directory held at that moment, not the true tip at deploy time). Meanwhile G-108's migration 005 (the `plan_review_findings_absence_required_check` CHECK constraint) WAS applied to the production schema (doc-repo-33 verified it live while building G-108). Net effect: new-code-missing-but-new-constraint-present, so every `seedMatrix` insert for a freshly created engagement violated the constraint. Confirmed live by the planner: reproduced on the broken revision (`POST /api/plan-review/engagements` → `violates check constraint`), then diagnosed by deploying a fresh build from a clean `git checkout --detach origin/main` with temporary row-level logging (`console.error` before each `upsertFinding`, on a 0%-traffic canary tag `diag-g110`) — every row logged a correctly non-null `absence` for its `Unchecked` determination, proving the merged code was already correct and the live revision was simply stale. Diagnostic logging stripped (`git diff --stat` against `origin/main` confirmed byte-identical before redeploy), rebuilt clean, smoke-tested on a second 0%-traffic tag (`g110-fix`: real parcel 201, malformed parcel 201, DEPLOY-7 fabrication check still holds, G-108 editions route 200), then shifted to 100% (`plan-review-00019-puj`) and reconfirmed on the production URL. **A real, separate bug also found and left in place, not fixed here:** `mcpCall`'s `textPayload()` (`src/mcp.mjs`) only checks the JSON-RPC-level `rpc.error`, never the tool-level `result.isError` — an MCP tool call that fails at the tool layer (e.g., `get_property_atom_chain` given a malformed `parcel_node_id`) returns `{text: "MCP error ..."}` rather than throwing, so `matrixFromChain`'s `sourceUnreachable = Boolean(chain?.error)` never fires for this failure class. Currently harmless only because every downstream row-builder still produces a correctly-typed `Unchecked`+absence regardless of which branch it takes — but the SOURCE_UNAVAILABLE absence kind, which exists specifically to distinguish "corpus unreachable" from "corpus reached, nothing there," never fires when it should. Named as a residual, not dispatched. **Operational lesson for the standing decisions:** `gcloud run deploy --source .` builds from local working-directory contents, not the git tip at deploy time — a deploy run immediately after a merge, from a checkout that has not re-fetched a concurrently-landed second merge, silently ships stale code even though the operator/agent believes they just deployed "current main." Worth a `git fetch && git log -1 --format=%H HEAD..origin/main` (expect empty) check before every `--source .` deploy, not just before the first checkout of a session. | Operator: "fix" | nick; doc-repo-1d (diagnosis, fix, deploy, verify) |

| A-097 | 2026-09-02 | **A-093's root cause CORRECTED, not just restated.** doc-repo-25 (who ran the G-107 deploy A-093 described as "stale") pushed back with a specific, checkable claim: it fetched and checked out `origin/main` immediately before deploying, and per A-093's own commit order (G-107 merged before G-108), a correct fetch at that moment would have captured only G-107. Checked the actual evidence rather than picking a side: the broken revision's Cloud Run build-source upload timestamp is `2026-09-02T15:48:30.820Z`. G-108 (#10) merged `2026-09-02 10:48:28 -0500` = `15:48:28Z`. G-107 (#9) merged `15:47:02Z`. **The gap between G-108 merging and the source upload is 2.8 seconds.** `gcloud run deploy --source .` tars and uploads the working directory as a step, taking real wall-clock time after the checkout completes — so doc-repo-25's fetch+checkout almost certainly happened at or before the instant G-108 merged, not carelessly stale. **A-093's "didn't re-fetch" framing was wrong; the corrected lesson: a deploy pipeline has a non-zero window between reading source and the build completing, and a concurrent merge landing inside that window is missed regardless of fetch discipline immediately prior — this is a property of the pipeline, not a lapse by the operator running it.** The practical fix is verifying what's actually running post-deploy (a commit/build marker, or exactly the diagnostic-canary technique A-093 used), not trusting a pre-deploy fetch as sufficient when merges are landing within the same ~90 seconds. doc-repo-25's separate hypothesis — that G-108's schema constraint was applied to production out-of-band, ahead of G-108's own code merge — is plausible and would also explain the break, but is **not verified**: no direct timestamp evidence for when migration 005 actually landed on the schema was checked by either party. Left as an open, named hypothesis, not asserted as fact. The associated cross-session memory (`gcloud-source-deploy-stale-local-checkout.md`) is corrected to match. | doc-repo-25 (pushback, verification); nick (none — planner self-correction) | nick; doc-repo-1d (corrected timeline, re-verification) |

| A-105 | 2026-09-02 | **CORRECTION to A-102/A-103/A-104: "no real per-section ICC content exists anywhere in the system" was WRONG.** Real, licensed IBC 2018 section content exists — 4,825 code-section atoms (`did:hauska:code-section:icc-model-code/2018-international-building-code-6th-printing/*`), fetched 2026-07-06 via the `icc-code-connect` adapter under the live ICC Code Connect PoC credential (GCP secret `ICC_CODE_CONNECT_CLIENT_ID`/`_SECRET`, entitled through Dec 30 2026), each carrying a real title, our own analysis text, a real source deep-link to `codes.iccsafe.org`, correctly withholding verbatim body text per the PoC license (`75n_icc_code_connect_catalog.md`). Live-verified this session: `get_atom` via `hauska-mcp-server` on `did:hauska:code-section:icc-model-code/2018-international-building-code-6th-printing/1001-1` (a real section, "General.", Chapter 10 Means of Egress) returns the full atom with a fresh `readContract`. **What actually happened and why A-102/A-103 got it wrong:** the test run there queried section `R311.7` — a genuinely non-existent IBC section number (R-prefix is IRC convention; the IBC uses numeric section ids like `1001.1`, confirmed by every real atom just read). Two stale fixture rows using that exact wrong pairing (`R311.7`, `R302.1` mislabeled as IBC) were found and correctly DELETED from `plan-review/src/code-lookup.mjs`'s `CODE_BOOKS.IBC2018P6.sections` on 2026-08-24 (predates this session). Testing a section number that was never real was guaranteed to return absence regardless of whether the corpus held anything — that result was real but proved nothing about the corpus. Separately, and this is the part A-102/A-103 never checked: plan-review's `CODE_BOOKS.IBC2018P6.sections` is `Object.freeze({})` — a static, hand-maintained, currently-empty manifest that has no query path into the substrate's atoms store at all. So plan-review's route returning absence is real and currently correct for EVERY IBC section, not because the content doesn't exist, but because nothing in plan-review's code ever asks the substrate for it. **The error was generalizing from one route's output to a claim about the whole system's data, instead of checking the substrate directly** — the same shape of mistake ENFORCEMENT.md's "read the authoritative record, never a proxy for it" names. Caught this session while scoping the actual next-push work, not by a downstream reviewer. **Consequence for G-110/G-112's residuals:** the "content-acquisition gap" language in A-103/A-104 is corrected to a wiring gap — the content is already acquired, licensed, and live-retrievable; what plan-review lacks is a lookup path from `CODE_BOOKS` into the substrate's real code-section atoms. This is now scoped as real, boundable engineering work for the next push, not open-ended data acquisition. See `_decisions/2026-09-02_plan_review_leads_the_bastrop_push.md`. | Operator: none this row (self-correction, surfaced while scoping "get the ICC demo functioning" per the operator's direct instruction) | nick; doc-repo-1d (found, live-verified, corrected) |

(3) **Found while scoping the above, a third, unrelated bug with the same symptom the operator actually reported:** every one of the 24 static nav hrefs shipped in `index.html` (every lens, every work item, every tab) carries no `cityKey`, so clicking ANY sidebar link dropped the visitor back onto the default pack — invisible for the one pack that used to exist (the default IS what the resolver falls back to with no query at all) and exactly what the operator saw as "my login went away" on `bastrop_tx` (the Hauska key itself never left `localStorage`; the CITY selection was what reset). ## Assumptions carried (each reversible by one amendment)

1. **The rebuild named in `34_smartcity_smart_files_and_foundation.md` open-item 1 IS this program.** The operator was uncertain. If it names a different effort, amend the G-03 and G-14 scope.
2. ~~**Smart Files is a rendering pass, not a build.**~~ **FALSIFIED 2026-08-14 by G-03; see A-002.** Smart Files is a BUILD. Retained struck-through rather than deleted: the assumption was written to be tested, the test ran, and the mechanism working is the record worth keeping. Doc 34 asserted it, the schema refuted it.
3. **Sensors are Asset Management Tier 2, not a fifth lane.** Ruled from the doc 32 Tier 2 section plus the sensor overview; both describe telemetry identically. UNTESTED — no row currently falsifies or confirms it.

**On carrying assumptions.** A-002 is the argument for this section existing. The planner carried doc 34's self-assessment into a program assumption; a read-only audit falsified it in one day and the cost was one amendment instead of a lane built on sand. Every future assumption added here is written to be falsifiable and named in a row that tests it.
